## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — NU

## 1. Owning the Misses

`01_management-and-track-record` found no mature missed management commitment: the two completed operating commitments were met, while FY2026 efficiency, U.S. investment and tax-rate objectives remain open. That limits the direct “owning a miss” test. The Q1 2026 revenue and GAAP-EPS shortfalls below Capital IQ consensus are useful context, but they are not misses against company guidance because NU gives no formal revenue or EPS target.

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| No mature management-guidance miss identified | Cost-to-serve and the earlier efficiency-normalisation commitment were met; current FY2026 targets have not reached their measurement date. | Not applicable; the direct test is limited by the small promise sample. | [Management & Track Record — NU, §4, 2026-08-30; Q1 2026 earnings call, Q&A — efficiency-ratio question] |
| Q1 2026 Street shortfall — context only, not company guidance | Revenue was US$4,967.967m versus US$5,059.867m consensus (−1.8%) and GAAP EPS US$0.1776 versus US$0.18736 (−5.3%). The call did not compare against consensus; it disclosed the managerial-P&L/IFRS distinction and discussed higher provisions and timing effects. | Not assessable as an owned management miss: NU had supplied no formal revenue or EPS guidance to revise. | [Capital IQ Estimates — Surprise, Fiscal Quarters, Q1 2026 — vendor export; Q1 2026 earnings call, opening disclosures and prepared remarks] |
| Q1 2026 17.6% efficiency ratio — unusually favorable result, not a miss | CFO Lago said about two-thirds of the Q1 result was expense timing, not recurring performance, and guided FY2026 toward about 20%. Q2 came in at 19.5% as the deferred marketing and real-estate spending reversed. | Owned plainly; this is the strongest evidence that management did not treat a favorable quarterly outcome as a new baseline. | [Q1 2026 earnings call, Q&A — efficiency-ratio question; Q2 2026 earnings call, prepared remarks] |

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | Adjusted Net Income adds back share-based compensation every year: US$289.3m in FY2023, US$408.2m in FY2024 and US$359.0m in FY2025. Stock compensation is a recurring employee cost, not a one-time event. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5A, Non-IFRS Financial Measures and Reconciliations; Earnings Quality — NU, §4] |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | FY2025 net income attributable to the parent was US$2,868.9m and Adjusted Net Income was US$3,072.6m: a US$203.7m, or 7.1%, uplift after tax and hedge effects. NU does not disclose adjusted EBITDA or adjusted EBIT. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5A, Adjusted Net Income reconciliation; Earnings Quality — NU, §7] |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | Yes, moderately. The filing gives a reconciliation and says the measure is not a substitute for IFRS profit, which is good disclosure; nevertheless, excluding a recurring employee cost makes adjusted profit less representative of the full economic cost of producing it. The 7.1% FY2025 net uplift is below the 15% size threshold used in the earnings-quality read. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Presentation of Financial Information — Special Note Regarding Non-IFRS Financial Measures; Earnings Quality — NU, §§4 and 9] |

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

NU has kept its main customer, monthly-active-customer, activity-rate, Monthly ARPAC and monthly-cost-to-serve KPIs through the FY2021–FY2025 annual filings, with definitions and cautions stated; the FY2025 Managerial P&L was disclosed as a complementary framework that preserves IFRS net income, cash flow and capital, not as a KPI rebase. [FY2021 Form 20-F, Presentation of Financial Information — Customers and Active Customers; FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5A, Managerial P&L]

The limitation is material: the CEO/CODM reports the group as one IFRS operating segment, so investors do not receive a country- or product-level profit-and-loss account even though the filing supplies product information and geographical revenue/assets. [FY2025 Form 20-F, Note 34, Segment information, F-97] The H1 2026 statements disclose both the US$991.0m deferred-tax benefit and negative CFO, but the reviewed Q2 prepared remarks and Q&A did not address either item; this is a presentation gap, not a contradiction with the filed numbers. [H1 2026 Unaudited Interim Condensed Consolidated Financial Statements, Statements of Income, p.5; Note 30, p.37; Q2 2026 earnings call, prepared remarks and Q&A]

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

Q1 was a Street miss but still a profitable quarter. The CEO’s language was promotional — calling it “another clean expression” of the model and saying higher provisions showed no deterioration — but the same call gave the three stated provision drivers and the CFO gave a numeric timing bridge for the unusually low efficiency ratio. [Q1 2026 earnings call, prepared remarks and Q&A]

The follow-through supports part of that explanation: Q2 operating expenses rose 20% sequentially as the delayed expenses landed, the ratio moved to 19.5%, and management restated the roughly 20% FY2026 target. [Q2 2026 earnings call, prepared remarks] In the reviewed Q2 Q&A, the CFO answered the Desenrola contribution directly (about 5% of cost of credit, a minority of the 115bp cost-of-credit contribution), while management declined to give an invented 2027 growth number; no Q&A deflection was evidenced in those exchanges. [Q2 2026 earnings call, Q&A — Goldman Sachs questions]

## 5. Candor Read

**Verdict: mixed.** Management’s clearest positive signal is its contemporaneous disclosure that two-thirds of Q1’s unusually low efficiency ratio was timing, followed by the expected Q2 reversal. [Q1 2026 earnings call, Q&A — efficiency-ratio question; Q2 2026 earnings call, prepared remarks]

The counterweight is recurring share-based-compensation exclusion from Adjusted Net Income and an earnings-call narrative that did not address the material H1 cash-flow and deferred-tax bridge; detailed IFRS notes prevent this from being an opaque filing record, but it does not support a high-trust conclusion. [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5A, Adjusted Net Income reconciliation; H1 2026 Unaudited Interim Condensed Consolidated Financial Statements, Statements of Income, p.5; Note 30, p.37]

The single biggest candor signal is the recurring US$359.0m FY2025 stock-compensation addback (US$203.7m net of tax/hedge effects), because it turns a normal recurring cost into “adjusted” profit despite an otherwise transparent reconciliation. [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 5A, Adjusted Net Income reconciliation]

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

Phase 1 recipes run: D-1, D-2, D-3 (filing/official-registry substitute), D-4, D-5, D-8 and D-9; the Cayman historic-name registry was not publicly available, so that one source is coverage-limited rather than assumed clear.

Phase 2 ran on one corroborated operating-origin entity, Nu Pagamentos; it identified the company’s self-disclosed founding trio and reached hop 2 through NuPay for Business. It terminated on `no_new_subjects` after the registry/regulator checks available in this run; 18 named entity branches remain declared below, rather than being represented as cleared.

## 3B. Lineage & Phoenix Read (A17-02, A17-03)

Nu Holdings was incorporated in Cayman in 2016, while the company’s own site says Nubank began in 2013. Official Brazilian public data shows Nu Pagamentos opened on 2013-06-04 and the annual filing identifies it as an indirect operating subsidiary. This reconciles the founding-year gap as a disclosed operating-company / later holding-company structure, not a proven predecessor or phoenix event. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4A, p.10 and Note 1; Brazil Portal da Transparência, CNPJ 18.236.120/0001-58, accessed 2026-08-30]

No evidence in the sources reviewed shows that a failed or struck-off predecessor transferred assets, brands, staff, address, products or customers into Nu Holdings without disclosed consideration. The group’s principal NUBANK mark is held by Nu Pagamentos, a group entity, not an outside controller-linked entity. [INPI, process 925913570, 2024-10-15; FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4B, pp.130–131]

## 6. Provenance & Next Data Request

- Filings supplied 32 subjects; discovery added Edward Wible and NuPay for Business; no user-supplied subject was used.
- Independent directors Anita Sands, David Marcus, Douglas Leone, Diego Piacentini, Jacqueline Reses, Luis Moreno, Rogério Peres and Thuan Pham are excluded from beneficial-interest scope only; each remains in the Tier-C integrity sweep.
- No aggregator roster conflict was resolved by dropping a subject. The only unresolved identity limitation is the unavailable historic Cayman registry for the parent/vehicle trail.
- **Single highest-value next data request:** a verified corporate-registry and beneficial-owner extract for Rua California Ltd. and the Junqueira/Pereira holder vehicles, with their incorporation jurisdiction and historical directors.

## 7. People & Network Integrity Read

All 24 people are graded Clean on their own record, but 19 are only Tier-B/C scoped and several identity-specific databases could not be completed. The riskiest network item is not a person: Nu Pagamentos has a small 2020 BCB fine and an ongoing ANPD monitoring entry; the former is adjudicated but low-value, and the latter is not a finding of liability. The discovery loop reached hop 2 and reconciled the 2013 operating origin with the 2016 Cayman parent; it stopped with 18 named entity branches declared rather than cleared. No open §24 Filter 1 integrity signal is proven, but the incompleteness of the holder-vehicle and regional-registry work means a rational minority holder should treat this as a provisional, coverage-limited integrity read, not an all-clear.

## management-governance / 08_audit-and-assurance-quality.md

_Source: `08_audit-and-assurance-quality.md`_

# Audit & Assurance Quality — NU

## 1. The Assurance Stack

| Role | Firm / Person | Network | Signing partner | Appointed | Tenure (yrs) | Local cap | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Statutory / group auditor | KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda. | KPMG International member firm | Rodrigo de Mattos Lia | Auditor since 2018 | 8 to FY2025 | Brazil: financial-institution audit firms rotate after five years; the filing says up to ten years where a statutory audit committee is in place. The filing does not expressly evidence the extension condition. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, F-2–F-5; Item 4.B, Brazil regulatory overview; FY2025 audited consolidated financial statements, Independent Auditors’ Report, pp.1–6] |
| Principal / component auditors | Not disclosed separately. KPMG states it directs, supervises and performs the group audit and remains solely responsible for its opinion; it does not give a component-auditor list or coverage percentages. | N/D | N/D | N/D | N/D | N/A | [FY2023 audited consolidated financial statements, Independent Auditors’ Report, auditor responsibilities, p.5; FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 4.B — Brazil, Mexico and Colombia operations] |
| Internal auditor | Internal-audit function; person / firm not disclosed in the filing reviewed. Audit & Risk Committee oversees it. | N/D | N/D | N/D | N/D | Brazil’s regulated entities require an internal-audit function. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 16A — Audit & Risk Committee duties; Item 4.B — Brazil regulatory overview] |
| Secretarial auditor (India) | Not applicable: NU is a Cayman-incorporated NYSE foreign private issuer with Brazilian operating centre, not an Indian issuer subject to MR-3. | N/A | N/A | N/A | N/A | India MR-3 regime does not apply. | [Governance Data Triage — NU, §1, 2026-08-30] |
| Audit & Risk Committee chair | Rogério Paulo Calderón Peres | N/A | N/A | Current in FY2025 filing | N/D | Committee appoints, compensates and oversees the independent auditor and pre-approves permitted services. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Items 6.A and 16A] |

## 2. Opinions, Controls & Restatements (A4-05, A4-09, A7-02)

| FY | Opinion (standalone) | Opinion (consolidated) | EoM / going concern | IFC/ICFR opinion | Restatement / prior-period adjustment | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| FY2023 | Not separately presented in the FPI annual filing (consolidated-report regime). | Unqualified: true and fair view under IFRS. | None identified in the report read. | ICFR attestation not separately located in the FY2023 source read. | No restatement identified in the report read; the five-year test is not fully evidenced. | Green for the consolidated opinion; ICFR / five-year restatement horizon incomplete. | [FY2023 audited consolidated financial statements, Independent Auditors’ Report, pp.1–5] |
| FY2024 | Not separately presented in the FPI annual filing (consolidated-report regime). | Unqualified: fair presentation under IFRS. | None identified in the report read. | ICFR attestation not separately located in the FY2024 source read. | FY2024 adoption of SAB 122 was an accounting-policy change, not an error correction, per the disqualifier scan; no material restatement identified. | Green for the consolidated opinion; ICFR / five-year restatement horizon incomplete. | [FY2024 audited consolidated financial statements, Independent Auditors’ Report, pp.1–6; Business-Model Disqualifier Scan — NU, audit/restatement hard-lock rows] |
| FY2025 | Not separately presented in the FPI annual filing (consolidated-report regime). | Unqualified: fair presentation under IFRS. | No qualification, emphasis-of-matter or going-concern paragraph identified. | KPMG opined ICFR was effective; management also concluded it effective, with no 2025 ICFR change materially affecting, or reasonably likely to materially affect, ICFR. | The Form 20-F cover says error-correction restatement was not required; Item 6.F says no compensation recovery was required for a restatement. This does not by itself prove a full five-year negative history. | Green for current opinion and ICFR. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, F-2–F-5; Item 15.B–D, pp.239–240; cover page; Item 6.F] |
| H1 FY2026 | N/A — interim consolidated report. | Unmodified review conclusion on the interim condensed consolidated financial statements. A review is narrower than an audit. | None identified in the review report. | Not an annual ICFR opinion. | N/A | Green, within limited-review scope. | [H1 2026 Form 6-K, Independent Auditors’ Review Report, filed 2026-08-13] |

**Hard-lock deference.** The disqualifier scan’s verdict is: no audit qualification or going-concern issue, no repeated auditor changes, and no material restatement in its assessed window. This report does not re-decide those locks; it records the surrounding assurance evidence. [Business-Model Disqualifier Scan — NU, hard-lock table, 2026-08-30]

## 3. Calibre, Rotation & Regulator Discipline (A4-01, A4-02, A4-13)

| Test | Raw value | Green band | Red band | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A4-01 — tenure vs local cap; rotation real? | KPMG has been auditor since 2018 (eight audit years through FY2025). No outgoing/incoming firm occurred in FY2023–FY2025, hence no same-network swap. Brazil’s stated five-year firm rotation can extend to ten years with a statutory audit committee; the filing does not expressly establish that condition. | Terms compliant; rotation orderly and real. | Same-network affiliate swap, or cap dodged via loopholes. | **Amber.** No rotation dodge is evidenced, but an eight-year tenure cannot be called locally compliant without evidence of the stated extension condition. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Report of Independent Registered Public Accounting Firm, F-5; Item 4.B, Brazil regulatory overview] |
| A4-02 — firm calibre vs company complexity | Big Four Brazilian KPMG firm, PCAOB-registered, group auditor of a multinational financial-services company. The signing partner is Rodrigo de Mattos Lia. The firm has PCAOB disciplinary orders, including a 2025 censure, penalty and remedial actions. | Big-4 / peer-reviewed national firm, listed practice sized to the company. | Firm too small for the complexity, or a firm/partner with disciplinary history. | **Red — RF-AUD-007, High.** Size and brand meet the capacity test, but the firm’s regulator-discipline history defeats a Green conclusion. The order does not say NU’s audit was one of the deficient engagements. | [FY2025 audited consolidated financial statements, Independent Auditors’ Report, pp.1–6; PCAOB Release No.105-2025-008, 2025-03-11] |
| A4-13 — audit-regulator orders vs firm or signing partner | PCAOB censured KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda., imposed a US$700,000 civil penalty and remedial actions in 2025 for audit-committee communication, Form 2/Form AP and quality-control failures in other issuer audits. PCAOB firm profile lists 2025, 2023 and 2017 disciplinary releases. Exact-name PCAOB/CVM web searches for Rodrigo de Mattos Lia produced no attributable order, but Brazil’s CFC/CRC disciplinary search was not available in the reviewed sources. | No orders against either firm or partner. | Active order or debarment against either; firm/partner regulator order triggers RF-AUD-007. | **Red — RF-AUD-007, High.** Firm discipline is proved; partner clearance is coverage-limited, not a clean result. There is no evidence in the order that NU itself was audited deficiently. | [PCAOB Release No.105-2025-008, 2025-03-11; PCAOB Firm Summary — KPMG Auditores Independentes Ltda., Firm No.1124, accessed 2026-08-30; Sweep Log] |

## 4. Economic Independence — Fees (A4-06, A4-07)

| FY | Audit fee | Non-audit fee (incl. network firms) | Non-audit / audit (%) | Prohibited services? | Fee vs peer median | Revenue growth | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|---|---|
| FY2024 | US$1.928m | US$0.060m (audit-related US$0.049m; tax US$0.005m; all other US$0.007m) | 3.1% | None disclosed. The fee note does not separately prove whether all network-firm billings are included. | Not assessable: no size-matched peer fee data in the pool. | N/A for base year | **Green for the disclosed non-audit ratio; A4-07 insufficient data on peer level.** | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 16C, p.241] |
| FY2025 | US$2.923m | US$0.227m (audit-related US$0.118m; tax US$0.009m; all other US$0.099m) | 7.8% | None disclosed. “All other” comprises ESG/sustainability assurance; no SOX §201 prohibited service is named. | Not assessable: no size-matched peer fee data in the pool. | 37.0% (US$15.775bn vs US$11.517bn) | **Green for A4-06. Amber for A4-07:** audit fee rose 51.6%, faster than revenue, so no low-balling signal; peer-median comparison remains unavailable. | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 16C, p.241; Statements of Income, F-6] |

The disclosed fee mix does not show economic dependence on consulting: FY2025 non-audit fees were US$226.8k / US$2,923.2k = **7.8%**, below the 25% Green band. The conclusion applies to disclosed categories only; the note does not separately state network-wide billing completeness. All auditor engagements for audit and permitted non-audit services require Audit & Risk Committee pre-approval. [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 16C, p.241]

## 5. Component & Subsidiary Coverage (A4-03, A4-14)

However, the public FPI filings reviewed do not provide a complete three-year named company-secretary/internal-audit exit timeline, so a full no-cluster conclusion is not proven. No RF-AUD-001 or RF-AUD-006 is triggered on available evidence.

## 10. Read

**Verdict: mixed — watch the named link.** NU’s consolidated annual opinions for FY2023–FY2025 are unqualified, FY2025 ICFR is effective, H1 FY2026 received an unmodified review conclusion, and disclosed non-audit fees are only 7.8% of audit fees. The weakest link is the incumbent firm: KPMG Brazil received a PCAOB censure, US$700,000 penalty and remedial actions in 2025 for failures in other issuer audits; the order does not identify NU, but it prevents a high-trust assurance conclusion. Trust would rise with evidence that the Brazil rotation extension applies, a component-auditor / coverage schedule, named internal-audit leadership, and current private-session / attendance disclosure.

## management-governance / 09_related-party-and-group-forensics.md

_Source: `09_related-party-and-group-forensics.md`_

# Related-Party & Group Forensics — NU

## 1. RPT Intensity & the Applicable Regime (A5-01)

Nu is a Cayman Islands foreign private issuer listed on the NYSE and reports under IFRS. The applicable disclosure read is Form 20-F Item 7.B and IAS 24 Note 28, not the India LODR Reg. 23 slabs or the domestic-U.S. Item 404 $120,000 rule. For Nu's regulated Brazilian entities, CMN Resolution 4,693 requires related-party credit transactions to be market-compatible and without extra benefits, subject to specified exceptions; it also requires an internal related-party policy. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.B — “Transactions with Related Parties”]

The filing does not disclose an annual aggregate of all related-party transaction flows. It separately reports key-management compensation and, for the unnamed director-linked company, a closing liability rather than an annual transaction amount. These are different bases and are not added together. The $0.926m closing liability is only 0.0059% of FY25 revenue, 0.0323% of FY25 net income, and 0.0012% of FY25 total assets; it is not evidence of a $0.926m annual outflow. [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 28, F-76–F-77; FY25 Form 20-F, Statements of Financial Position, F-8–F-9; earnings/01, FY25 income statement]

| FY | Aggregate RPTs ex-dividends | % of revenue | % of PAT | % of assets | Material-RPT line that year | Above the line? | Minority-approved? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2023 | Not assessable: Note 27 says no transactions with other related parties; key-management compensation is separately disclosed, but director/family lending amounts are not | N/A | N/A | N/A | Form 20-F Item 7.B / IAS 24 disclosure; no aggregate approval line stated | Not assessable | Not disclosed | Amber | [FY23 Form 20-F, Note 27 — Related parties] |
| 2024 | Not assessable: $1.795m other-related-party **liability** is disclosed, not an annual flow; key-management compensation is separate | N/A | N/A | N/A | Form 20-F Item 7.B / IAS 24 disclosure; no aggregate approval line stated | Not assessable | Not disclosed | Amber | [FY24 Form 20-F, Note 29 — Related parties] |
| 2025 | Not assessable: $0.926m other-related-party **liability** and $91.269m aggregate management compensation are disclosed on different bases | N/A | N/A | N/A | Form 20-F Item 7.B / IAS 24 disclosure; Brazil CMN 4,693 governs regulated-subsidiary credit terms | Not proven | Not disclosed | Amber | [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 28, F-76–F-77; Item 4.B — Transactions with Related Parties] |

The business-model hard-lock scan records no proven RPT sales/revenue or purchases/expenses ratio above 25%; it correctly treats the $0.926m liability as neither a sales nor an expense numerator. This report does not re-adjudicate that lock. [business-model/01_disqualifier-scan, RPT hard-lock table]

(group entity) | Owner of the Brazilian NUBANK word mark; the Form 20-F says the group owns Nu/Nubank marks in relevant markets | No group royalty disclosed | N/A | Brand ownership is identified; no related-party brand fee is disclosed | Not assessable because no fee is disclosed | Not disclosed / no RPT vote reported | Green for the identified group-owned brand; scope is limited to disclosed arrangements | [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.B — Intellectual Property; management-governance/07, Section 0A] |

## 4. Round-Tripping Cross-Year Name Match (A5-04)

One list was built from every named FY22–FY25 RPT counterparty plus the scoped recently disclosed customer/supplier graph. No name appears as both a customer and a vendor in the available set. This is a coverage-limited result, not proof that round-tripping is absent: the customer/supplier export is not a full historical commercial ledger and values are unavailable. [FY22–FY25 Form 20-F related-party notes; relationships.json, scope_notes]

| Name | Years as customer | Years as vendor | Both roles? | Sales ≈ purchases (circular)? | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Circle Internet Group | Recent disclosed relationship only | None in supplied graph | No | Not assessable: no values | Green within supplied graph; scope limited | [relationships.json, Circle edge] |
| AWS; Computershare; Edenred; MasterCard; Mercedes-Benz Grand Prix; Numis; U.S. DFC; Wise | None in supplied graph | Recent disclosed relationship only | No | Not assessable: no values | Green within supplied graph; scope limited | [relationships.json, supplier edges] |
| Rodamoinho; Reprograma; unidentified director-linked company | None disclosed | None disclosed | No | Not applicable: no customer/vendor leg disclosed | Amber — commercial leg is absent or unnamed, so no circularity test is possible | [FY22–FY25 Form 20-F related-party notes] |

No both-role match is a circularity finding. A future match would be only an Amber lead unless matching amounts/timing, off-market pricing, or a funds-flow circle is evidenced.

## 6. Related-Party M&A — Including Aborted (A5-09)

| Deal (incl. proposed / withdrawn) | Seller / target link to promoter | Year | Value | Fairness opinion? | Outcome | Verdict | Source |

| Proposed acquisition of 100% of Banco Porto Real de Investimentos S.A. | Seller identity and any promoter/director relationship are not stated in the interim report | 2026 | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | Pending Brazilian Central Bank approval at the reporting date | Insufficient Data — not evidence of related-party M&A; seller, price, and conflict review remain open | [H1 2026 Interim Report, Note 35(b) — Subsequent Events, 2026-08-14] |

Pool review covered FY23–FY25 Form 20-F related-party disclosures, the H1 2026 subsequent-events note, and the CIQ timeline/M&A extracts. The pool did not contain a complete board-outcome, AGM/postal-ballot, or exchange-announcement archive, and no live database search was performed; therefore this is not presented as a complete no-hit sweep for aborted deals. [FY23–FY25 Form 20-F related-party notes; H1 2026 Interim Report, Note 35(b); CIQ Corporate Timeline/M&A extracts — Tier 5]

Structure read: the listed Cayman parent is the capital-market holding company; Brazilian, Mexican, and Colombian regulated subsidiaries operate payments, lending, brokerage, and investment activities; lower-tier entities include product/technology and investment-management businesses. The Form 20-F says the group contains 45 subsidiaries, while Exhibit 8.1 ends at 44 named rows and CIQ's narrower current operating-unit view has 34. The filing does not reconcile these counts; this is an Amber disclosure follow-up, not proof of hidden entities. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.C; Exhibit 8.1; Note 3, F-19–F-20; CIQ Corporate Structure Tree, as of 2026-08-30 — Tier 5]

## 9. Cash & Funding Topology (A11-02, A11-03)

**Matched-basis rule.** The only reported cash number used below is a group consolidated, period-end balance. It is not divided by a parent-only or subsidiary-only figure, so no false cash-location ratio is presented.

| Question | Raw value | Basis (both sides) | Matched ratio | Verdict | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Where does consolidated cash sit (parent vs subs)? | $13.552bn consolidated cash and cash equivalents at 2026-06-30; parent/sub split not disclosed | Group period-end cash only; no matched parent and subsidiary balances | N/A | Amber — location is not visible. Regulated Brazilian, Mexican, and Colombian subsidiaries face restrictions on transfers to/from group entities and on settling other-group liabilities | [H1 2026 Interim Report, Statements of Financial Position, p.8; FY25 Form 20-F, Note 3, F-20] |
| Share held with a related finance company / treasury vehicle | Not disclosed | No cash-by-entity or related-finance-company balance | N/A | Insufficient Data | [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 3, F-19–F-20] |
| Parent borrowing while subs hold the cash? | Not assessable | No matched parent borrowing and subsidiary cash figures | N/A | Insufficient Data — group cash and group debt cannot answer a parent-versus-subsidiary topology question | [H1 2026 Interim Report, Statements of Financial Position, p.8] |
| ICDs to group entities — amount, rate, tenor | Not disclosed | Consolidated accounts eliminate balances; FX on intercompany loans remains in consolidation mechanics | N/A | Insufficient Data | [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 3, F-19–F-20] |
| Evergreen / rolled-over ICDs? | Not disclosed | No loan-level schedule | N/A | Insufficient Data | [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 3, F-19–F-20] |

The regulatory-transfer restriction is a fact about access to regulated-subsidiary assets. It is not evidence that cash is trapped, circularly moved, or misused; the parent/sub cash split needed to decide that is not disclosed. [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 3, F-20]

## 14. Read

No annual amount of value moving to a promoter or promoter-linked entity is proven from the available filings. The only quantified current non-management other-RPT item is a $0.926m closing liability tied to a conditional cash incentive received from an unnamed company led by a Nu director — 0.0323% of FY25 net income, and a balance rather than an annual cash outflow. [FY25 Form 20-F, Note 28(a), F-76; earnings/01, FY25 net income]

The main risk is disclosure granularity, not a proved leakage channel: the counterparty name, commercial terms, annual flow, director/KMP lending balances, and parent/subsidiary cash location are absent. The deepest observed group path is three layers and the disclosed-versus-vendor entity counts differ (45 subsidiaries in Item 4.C versus 34 current operating units in CIQ); this could make a small flow harder to trace, but does not itself establish hidden value transfer. The highest-value next disclosure is a named, annual, transaction-level RPT schedule covering the director-linked company and all director/KMP credit balances, rates, approvals, and repayments. [FY25 Form 20-F, Item 4.C; Notes 3 and 28; CIQ Corporate Structure Tree, as of 2026-08-30 — Tier 5]

# Contingent Liabilities & Commitments — NU

Nu Holdings Ltd. is a Cayman-incorporated NYSE foreign private issuer that reports under IFRS in U.S. dollars. Amounts below are US$ millions unless noted otherwise; the latest balance-sheet date is 30 June 2026. `balance-sheet-survival/05_off-balance-sheet-and-contingencies.md` is not available for this run, so this is this module's own filing-based read and does not attempt the survival module's maximum-exposure analysis.

## 1. Headline Exposure (A7a-01)

The quantified off-P&L litigation amount is US$9.675m: US$4.784m civil plus US$4.891m labor claims classified as possible. The US$44.343m provision is already recognized in liabilities, so it is shown separately rather than mislabelled as off-P&L. The computed US$54.018m “total disclosed legal exposure” is therefore a transparent build: US$44.343m provided + US$9.675m quantified possible. It excludes the Colombian GMF class action because the filing gives the US$2.5bn claim for all 52 defendants but says Nu Colombia's share cannot be quantified; allocating a share would be invention. It is also not established whether that case is included in the US$4.784m possible-civil line. [H1 FY2026 Interim Financial Statements, Note 25, pp.32–33; FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 8.A Legal Proceedings, p.207]

The current-period net-worth and PAT denominators come directly from the H1 primary statements because the available earnings historical-financials report does not publish a net-worth subtotal. The deterministic Capital IQ facts sidecar was read; it has no present contingent-liability, litigation, guarantee, or commitment fact, so no vendor number is substituted for the filing read.

## 2. Item-by-Item Register & Movement (A7a-02, A7a-13)

The first three rows are recognized provisions; the next two are quantified possible claims and are not provisioned. FY23–FY25 are year-end points; FY0 is 30 June 2026, so the last movement is a six-month bridge and must not be read as a full-year growth rate. The FY22 note was also read: total provision was US$17.947m, including a US$15.747m PIS/COFINS tax provision that was settled in 2023. [FY2022 Form 20-F, Note 23, pp.F-84–F-85]

| Item (as named in the note) | FY-3 FY2023 | FY-2 FY2024 | FY-1 FY2025 | FY0 H1 FY2026 | Additions / Reversals / Crystallized | Posture change (forum, last outcome) | Explained in narrative? | Verdict | Source |

| Colombian GMF class action — possible loss, Nu share unquantified | N/A | N/A | 2,500.000 claim for all 52 defendants; Nu share not quantified | Not repeated in H1 Note 25 | No provision or movement disclosed; a simple majority/52 allocation is not supportable because allocation is stated to depend on individual tax collection shares | Nu Colombia moved for dismissal; FY25 filing says the case was at an initial stage awaiting court resolution | Yes for the limits of knowledge; no current court result in pool | Amber | [FY2025 Form 20-F, Item 8.A Legal Proceedings, p.207] |

The aggregate recognized provision increased from US$22.551m at FY2024 to US$30.920m at FY2025 and US$44.343m at H1 FY2026. That is a rise in the balance, but it is not a flat-total trap: each component is bridged, the large FY23 tax provision actually crystallized, and there is no evidence of an item silently disappearing. There is also no evidence of repeated crystallization that would trigger RF-CL-003. The adverse caveat is different: current tax, civil, and labor cases are aggregated, so their appellate posture cannot be tested item by item.

## 3. Tax Disputes (A7a-03 direct, A7a-04 indirect)

Nu does not split the US$8.126m tax-risk provision between direct and indirect taxes, does not disclose the forum for the current provision, and does not separately identify a possible tax-loss amount. It would be incorrect to call either direct or indirect tax nil. The historical PIS/COFINS provision is the only individual tax case described: the FY2023 filing says it was settled through release of the judicial deposit to the Brazilian tax authority. [FY2023 Form 20-F, Note 24, pp.F-84–F-85]

## 11. Read

Quantified current legal exposure is US$54.018m — US$44.343m already provided plus US$9.675m quantified possible — only 0.41% of US$13.252bn net worth, so it clears the size band on the amounts Nu can actually quantify. The biggest unresolved item is the Colombian GMF class action: the FY2025 filing calls the US$2.5bn all-defendant claim possible and says Nu Colombia's share cannot be quantified, so it cannot honestly be sized against equity or assumed harmless. The provision record is mixed but not evasive on available evidence: it separately states the probable/possible policy, booked provisions grew with reconciled additions, reversals and payments, and the FY2022 PIS/COFINS provision crystallized through a disclosed 2023 settlement; no serial appellate losses carried unprovided are shown. A rational minority holder should demand the next filing state the GMF allocation, current court status, and whether it is included in the possible-civil subtotal, and should require a complete issued-guarantee, recourse-financing, statutory-dues and capital-commitments schedule.

## 2. Beneish M-Score (A8-14) — computed with python3, computation shown

Not applicable (sector): the Beneish M-score is a non-financial manipulation-screening model. Nu’s core assets and liabilities are credit receivables, loans, deposits and ECL allowances; applying sales/receivables, gross-margin, inventory and leverage components would produce a misleading score. [Management-governance MODULE_RULES.md, Financials overlay]

## 3. Dechow F-Score & Accrual Battery (A8-17, A8-18) — computed with python3, computation shown

Not applicable (sector): the Dechow F-score and RSST accruals battery are non-financial accounting-risk models and have the same limitation. No F-score, TATA or issuance-in-a-flagged-year conclusion is asserted. [Management-governance MODULE_RULES.md, Financials overlay]

## 10. Regulator-Found Divergence (A8-20) — swept per frameworks/GOVERNANCE_DATABASES.md

| Check | Finding | Red band | Verdict | Evidence |

## 12. Forensics Read

The non-financial Beneish and Dechow batteries are not valid for Nu, so no M- or F-score is asserted. The financials overlay finds no provision release funding profit, no material goodwill or capitalization problem, and no located regulator-found accounting divergence; FY25 cash taxes rose 30.0% while revenue rose 37.0%. The worst measured signal is credit-risk intensity: ECL rose from 15.37% of gross card and loan credit at FY25 to 16.86% at 2026-06-30 (+149bp), while H1 FY26 CFO was US$-1,242.0m versus US$1,932.5m net income. That is a lender credit-growth/funding warning, not evidence of manipulation; the low H1 ETR and new managerial P&L need the next audited disclosure to confirm durability and comparability.

The official sources therefore conflict on current status. | No monetary amount published | N/A | Medium uncertainty; no published sanction amount or final adverse finding located | No individual matter located in FY25 Form 20-F Item 8; materiality cannot be established from the public record | Amber — resolve ANPD status before any conclusion on active investigation | [ANPD, Processos de Fiscalização em Andamento, accessed 2026-08-30]; [ANPD, 3º Relatório de Ciclo de Monitoramento — 1º semestre de 2025, p. 35] |

## 9. Read

**Hygiene lapses, watch.** The public record adds three small historical BCB reporting fines at Nu Pagamentos, a still-unresolved Procon appeal outcome, and an ANPD status conflict to a filing record that describes the material litigation it identifies. Nu has a written whistleblower policy and describes an independent channel, but gives no complaint or resolution statistics, so machinery cannot be graded as proven in practice. Nothing in this sweep establishes material undisclosed litigation or a parent-level enforcement hard cap; however, incomplete court, NYSE, rating-agency and direct sanctions coverage floors the **Legal & regulatory risk at 40/100 (INVERTED — higher = worse)** and caps confidence at 70.
